<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the strong hyperkähler SYZ conjecture: every holomorphic line bundle with nonzero nef isotropic first Chern class on a compact irreducible holomorphic symplectic Kähler manifold is semiample.

- **claim-nef-isotropic-semiampleness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $X$ be a compact irreducible holomorphic symplectic Kähler manifold of complex dimension $2n$, where $n\ge1$. If a holomorphic line bundle $L$ satisfies $$c_1(L)\ne0,\qquad c_1(L)\in\overline{\mathcal K_X}, \qquad q_X(c_1(L))=0,$$ then $L$ is semiample.* *More precisely, there exist a positive integer $m$, a normal projective variety $B$, an ample line bundle $A$ on $B$, and a surjective holomorphic map with connected fibers $$f:X\longrightarrow B,\qquad L^m\simeq f^*A,$$ such that $\dim_{\mathbb C}B=n$. Every irreducible component of every fiber has complex dimension $n$, and $\sigma$ vanishes on its smooth locus.
  - Explanation: A prescribed nonzero nef isotropic line bundle becomes globally generated after taking a positive tensor power on the original manifold. Its associated map has a normal projective base and equidimensional Lagrangian fibers.
- **claim-smooth-deformation-finiteness** (classification, [source](#stmt-cor-finiteness)): For each integer $n\ge1$, there are only finitely many smooth deformation types of compact irreducible holomorphic symplectic Kähler manifolds of complex dimension $2n$ with $b_2\ge5$.
  - Explanation: In fixed complex dimension, the stated second-Betti-number hypothesis permits an isotropic lattice class to be found and deformed into a nef class. Semiampleness and external boundedness then give finiteness of smooth deformation types.
- **claim-projective-space-base** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-cor-projective-base)): In Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, assume additionally that $X$ is projective, and let $f:X\to B$ be the associated connected-fiber map furnished there. Then $B\simeq\mathbb P^n_{\mathbb C}$. Moreover, there do not exist a prime divisor $D\subset B$, an effective divisor $E$ on $X$, and an integer $r>1$ such that $f^*D=rE$.
  - Explanation: Projectivity of the total space strengthens the base description to complex projective space and excludes the specified divisorial multiplicities.
- **claim-maximal-crossing-special-lagrangian-torus** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-torus)): Let $\pi:\mathcal Y\to\Delta$ be the restriction to a disk of a projective algebraic family over a smooth curve. Assume that $\mathcal Y$ is smooth, $\mathcal Y_0$ is a reduced simple normal crossings divisor, and the fibers $Y_t$ for $t\ne0$ are smooth connected projective manifolds of complex dimension $d\ge2$ with trivial canonical bundle. Let $a_t$ be polarizing classes given by a line bundle on $\mathcal Y$; this line bundle need not be relatively ample at $0$. Let $\eta_t$ be nowhere vanishing holomorphic $d$-forms which extend as a section of the relative logarithmic canonical line bundle. Put $s=-\log\|t\|$, and suppose that, for constants $c,C>0$, $$c s^d\le I_t:=\int_{Y_t}\|\eta_t\|^2\le C s^d, \qquad 0<\|t\|\ll1. \label{eq:log-volume-growth}$$ Suppose, moreover, that at some point $o\in\mathcal Y_0$ exactly $d+1$ components meet and that the logarithmic coefficient of $\eta$ at $o$ is nonzero. Thus in a coordinate neighborhood of $o$, $$z_0z_1\cdots z_d=t, \qquad \eta_t=f(z)\,\frac{\mathrm dz_1}{z_1}\wedge\cdots\wedge \frac{\mathrm dz_d}{z_d}, \qquad f(o)\ne0. \label{eq:crossing-chart}$$ Equip $Y_t$ with the Ricci-flat Kähler metric in $a_t$. For every sufficiently small nonzero $t$, the fiber $Y_t$ contains a smooth embedded special Lagrangian real $d$-torus isotopic in $Y_t$ to an angular coordinate torus in <a href="#eq:crossing-chart" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:crossing-chart">[eq:crossing-chart]</a>. Here special Lagrangian means that the Kähler form restricts to zero and that $\eta_t$ has constant phase on the oriented torus.
  - Explanation: A polarized semistable Calabi–Yau degeneration with maximal logarithmic volume growth and a nonzero maximal-crossing coefficient contains special Lagrangian tori near the degeneration. Their coordinate isotopy class is retained.
- **claim-mixed-intersection-trace-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-mixed-estimates)): For every fixed $B\Subset\Sigma$ and $0\le k\le d$, $$\int_{\mathcal T_t(B)}\omega_\gamma^k\wedge\omega_t^{d-k} \le C_B s^d. \label{eq:mixed-estimates}$$ Consequently, with traces taken for real Riemannian metrics, $$\int_{\mathcal T_t(B)}\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_\gamma g_t\,\mathrm dV_\gamma\le C_Bs^d, \qquad \int_{\mathcal T_t(B)}\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_{g_t}\gamma\,\mathrm dV_t\le C_Bs^d. \label{eq:trace-estimates}$$
  - Explanation: Mixed intersections control the traces of the scaled Ricci-flat metric relative to the logarithmic flat metric in both directions. These integrated bounds support center selection and coordinate control.
- **claim-positive-fraction-noncollapsed-centers** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-many-noncollapsed-centers)): There exist constants $\delta,v>0$ and measurable sets $E_t\subset\mathcal T_t(B_0)$ such that $$\mu_t(E_t)\ge\delta\mu_t(Y_t),\qquad \mu_t(B_{g_t}(q,1))\ge v\quad(q\in E_t). \label{eq:many-noncollapsed-centers}$$ All balls here and below use the intrinsic distance of the complete manifold $(Y_t,g_t)$.
  - Explanation: A positive fraction of the total scaled volume consists of centers with a uniform intrinsic unit-ball volume lower bound. Coordinate boxes and volume comparison establish this without a diameter bound.
- **claim-vanishing-harmonic-map-energy** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-small-energy)): Let $h_\nu$ be metrics converging smoothly on a fixed smooth domain $D$ of real dimension $N$, and let $\psi_\nu:(D,h_\nu)\to P$ be smooth harmonic maps to a fixed compact Riemannian manifold. If their energies tend to zero on every relatively compact subdomain, then $\sup_K\|\mathrm d\psi_\nu\|\to0$ for every $K\Subset D$. After extraction they converge smoothly to a constant on each connected relatively compact domain.
  - Explanation: For smoothly converging domain metrics and a fixed compact target, vanishing local harmonic-map energy forces derivatives to vanish on interior compact sets. Subsequence extraction then yields smooth constant limits on connected domains.
- **claim-capacity-extension-derivative-bounds** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-capacity-extension)): Each $u_j$ extends to a weakly harmonic function in $W^{1,2}_{\mathrm{loc}}(Z)$. Moreover $\lvert\mathrm du_j\rvert\le C$ on $G$, with a constant independent of the point. Both $\mathrm dF$ and its local inverse derivatives are uniformly bounded.
  - Explanation: The limiting logarithmic functions extend weakly harmonically across the possible singular set. Their global derivative bounds and the positive coordinate Jacobian also bound the derivatives of the local coordinate inverse.
- **claim-regular-path-distance** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-regular-paths)): The intrinsic path distance on $G$ equals the restriction of $d_Z$. In particular $F$ extends to a Lipschitz map $F:Z\to H$.
  - Explanation: Paths in the regular set approximate ambient minimizing distances by avoiding the sufficiently small singular set. The coordinate map therefore extends Lipschitz continuously to the completion.
- **claim-completion-full-cylinder** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-full-cylinder)): The extension $F:Z\to H$ is a bi-Lipschitz homeomorphism.
  - Explanation: The coordinate inverse lifts paths outside the singular image and extends by completeness and density. This identifies the whole metric limit with the full cylinder by a bi-Lipschitz homeomorphism.
- **claim-actual-logarithmic-convergence** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-actual-log-convergence)): For every fixed $r>0$, the chart $\mathcal L_t$ contains $Q_r$ in its image for all sufficiently small $t$ along the selected subsequence. Its inverse $\Psi_t:Q_r\to Y_t$ satisfies $$\Psi_t^*g_t\longrightarrow g_\infty,\qquad \Psi_t^*\omega_t\longrightarrow\omega_\infty,\qquad \Psi_t^*\Omega_t\longrightarrow\Omega_\infty \quad\hbox{in }C^\infty_{\mathrm{loc}}(Q_r),$$ where the limits are constant tensors and $\Omega_\infty\ne0$. The angular coordinates of $\Psi_t$ are the original $\arg z_j$.
  - Explanation: After removing the apparent singularities, smooth convergence holds in the original logarithmic chart on each fixed cylinder tube. The angular marking remains unchanged, so a perturbed torus keeps its coordinate isotopy class.
- **claim-prescribed-cusp-family** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-polarized-family)): Put $d=2n$. There exist a primitive vector $h\in\Lambda$ with $q(h)>0$ and $q(h,e)=0$, a rational subspace $$P=\langle e,f,v\rangle_{\mathbb Q}\subset h^\perp, \qquad \operatorname{sign}(q\|_P)=(2,1),$$ and a projective semistable family $\pi:\mathcal Y\to\Delta$ with the following properties. Here $\Delta$ is a sufficiently small disk in a smooth algebraic curve, and $\Delta^*=\Delta\setminus\{0\}$.* 1. *The total space $\mathcal Y$ is smooth, the central fiber is a reduced simple normal crossings divisor, and every $Y_t$, $t\ne0$, is an irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifold in the marked deformation component of $X$. A line bundle $\mathcal A$ on $\mathcal Y$ restricts to an ample bundle on every $Y_t$ with $c_1(\mathcal A\|_{Y_t})=kh$ for a fixed positive integer $k$ under parallel transport. The family is the analytic restriction of an algebraic family.* 2. *The rational vectors can be chosen so that $$\label{eq:family-hyperbolic-basis} q(e)=q(f)=0,\quad q(e,f)=1,\quad q(e,v)=q(f,v)=0,\quad a:=q(v)>0.$$ On the universal cover of $\Delta^*$ the marked Hodge line is spanned by the normalized vector $$\label{eq:family-period} p(\tau)=f+\tau v-\frac a2\tau^2e, \qquad \operatorname{Im}\tau>0.$$ The function $\tau$ tends to the cusp represented by the original line $\mathbb Qe$, and $$\label{eq:family-period-identities} q(p(\tau))=0,\qquad q(p(\tau),e)=1,\qquad q(p(\tau),\overline{p(\tau)}) =2a(\operatorname{Im}\tau)^2.$$ For a consistent convention for second-cohomology monodromy there is a rational $w>0$ such that $$\label{eq:family-monodromy} Me=e,\qquad Mv=v-aw e,\qquad Mf=f+w v-\frac{aw^2}{2}e, \qquad M\|_{P^\perp}=1.$$ The orthogonal complement in the last formula is taken in $\Lambda_{\mathbb Q}$. In particular, $$\label{eq:family-monodromy-image} (M-1)P=\langle e,v\rangle_{\mathbb Q},\quad (M-1)^2f=-aw^2e\ne0,\quad (M-1)^3=0.$$ The same image statement holds if the monodromy convention uses $M^{-1}$.* 3. *There is a single-valued holomorphic relative symplectic form $\sigma_t$ on the smooth family, uniquely normalized by $q([\sigma_t],e)=1$. The forms $\eta_t=\sigma_t^n$ extend to a holomorphic section of the relative logarithmic top-form line bundle of the semistable family. With $s=-\log\|t\|$, $$\label{eq:family-volume-growth} c s^d\le \int_{Y_t}\|\eta_t\|^2\le C s^d$$ for positive constants $c,C$. There is a point $o\in Y_0$ where $d+1$ components meet and coordinates satisfying $$\label{eq:family-maximal-residue} t=z_0\cdots z_d,\qquad \eta=F(z)\,\frac{\mathrm dz_1}{z_1}\wedge\cdots\wedge \frac{\mathrm dz_d}{z_d},\qquad F(o)\ne0.$$* 4. *For any prescribed irrational number $\lambda$, there is a path tending to $0$ in $\Delta^*$, with a continuous choice of lift, along which $\tau=\lambda+iy$ and $y\to+\infty$.
  - Explanation: A projective semistable family realizes a cusp labelled by the supplied isotropic class. Its normalized periods, full second-cohomology monodromy, logarithmic volume growth, and maximal-crossing residue are controlled together.
- **claim-polarization-free-period-quotient** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-polarized-family-construction)): Let $X$ be a compact irreducible holomorphic symplectic Kähler manifold, fix its connected marked deformation component, and put $\Lambda=H^2(X,\mathbb Z)$ with its integral BBF form $q$. For each nonzero primitive $e\in\Lambda$ with $q(e)=0$, there are* 1. *a primitive $h\in\Lambda$ with $q(h)>0$ and $q(h,e)=0$, and a manifold in the chosen marked component on which $h$ is ample;* 2. *a smooth projective algebraic family over a smooth connected quasi-projective base $S$, whose fibers are irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifolds in that component, with a relative polarization of marked class $kh$ for one $k\in\mathbb Z_{>0}$;* 3. *a torsion-free finite-index subgroup $$\Gamma\subset G_h(\mathbb Z) :=\{g\in O(\Lambda,q):gh=h,\ gD_h=D_h\},$$ where $D_h$ is the component of the polarized period domain containing the chosen ample period, such that the family monodromy is contained in $\Gamma$ and the descended period map $$\Phi:S\longrightarrow Q_h:=\Gamma\backslash D_h$$ is algebraic. The quotient $Q_h$ is smooth and quasi-projective, $D_h\to Q_h$ is a covering map with free $\Gamma$-action, and a local marked lift of $\Phi$ is submersive at some point of $S$.* *More generally, conclusions <span class="upright">(2)</span> and <span class="upright">(3)</span> hold for any prescribed primitive positive $h\in\Lambda$ which is ample on a manifold in the chosen marked component. The markings are obtained by deformation and parallel transport from $X$. No finite-index assertion about the image of the family monodromy inside $\Gamma$ is part of this construction.
  - Explanation: A supplied primitive null vector admits an orthogonal positive polarization on a deformation. An algebraic polarized family and a free arithmetic period quotient retain the full lattice marking without assuming a finite-index family-monodromy image.
- **claim-torus-on-prescribed-irrational-ray** (construction, [source](#stmt-cor-family-torus)): Fix an irrational $\lambda$ and choose the lifted path in Proposition <a href="#prop:family" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:family">[prop:family]</a> with $\tau=\lambda+iy$. For every sufficiently large $y$, the corresponding smooth fiber contains an embedded special Lagrangian real $d$-torus for its Ricci-flat metric in the polarization class. This torus is isotopic to a coordinate angle torus in the chart <a href="#eq:family-maximal-residue" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:family-maximal-residue">[eq:family-maximal-residue]</a>. The period and monodromy on that fiber are the exact marked data <a href="#eq:family-period" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:family-period">[eq:family-period]</a>–<a href="#eq:family-monodromy-image" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:family-monodromy-image">[eq:family-monodromy-image]</a>.
  - Explanation: The prescribed-cusp family satisfies the special Lagrangian torus theorem along the chosen irrational vertical period ray. The torus and exact marked monodromy data are obtained on the same fiber.
- **claim-cohomological-hyperkahler-rotation** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-rotation-criterion)): Let $(Y,g,I,J,K)$ be a hyperkähler manifold of real dimension $4n$, where $n\ge1$ and $IJ=K$, and let $i:T\hookrightarrow Y$ be a compact, connected, oriented embedded special Lagrangian submanifold for the complex structure $I$. If $i^*[\omega_K]=0$ in $H^2(T,\mathbb R)$, then $T$ is a complex $n$-dimensional submanifold for $J$ and is Lagrangian for $\omega_K+i\omega_I$. If $T$ is diffeomorphic to a real $2n$-torus, then it is a complex torus for $J$.
  - Explanation: Cohomological vanishing of the indicated Kähler form, together with special Lagrangian calibration, forces equality in Wirtinger’s inequality. The submanifold becomes holomorphic Lagrangian after rotation; torus topology then identifies it as a complex torus.
- **claim-nonprojective-torus-fibration** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-thm-nonprojective-torus)): If a nonprojective compact irreducible hyperkähler manifold $Z$ of complex dimension $2n$ contains a holomorphic Lagrangian complex torus $S$, then its algebraic dimension is $n$ and it has a holomorphic algebraic reduction $Z\to B$ which is a Lagrangian fibration with $S$ as a fiber. In particular, this fibration has normal projective base and provides a nontrivial semiample pullback bundle on $Z$.
  - Explanation: The stated external theorem turns a holomorphic Lagrangian complex torus on a nonprojective irreducible hyperkähler manifold into a holomorphic algebraic reduction and a nontrivial semiample pullback bundle.
- **claim-fixed-class-nef-semiample-locus** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-thm-fixed-class)): (Soldatenkov–Verbitsky (Soldatenkov and Verbitsky 2025, Theorem 3.7(v)) [[SV](#ref-SV)]). *With this notation, $$\mathcal T^\circ_{e,\mathrm{sa}}\ne\varnothing \quad\Longrightarrow\quad \mathcal T^\circ_{e,\mathrm{nef}} =\mathcal T^\circ_{e,\mathrm{sa}}. \label{eq:rotation-sv}$$
  - Explanation: The stated external deformation theorem propagates semiampleness from a semiample point to every nef point in the same positive fixed-class deformation locus. The class and its sign must be retained.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-polarized-family",
        "exact_quote": "For any prescribed irrational number $\\lambda$, there is a path tending to $0$ in $\\Delta^*$, with a continuous choice of lift, along which $\\tau=\\lambda+iy$ and $y\\to+\\infty$.",
        "source_id": "PM-041.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-polarized-family",
          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"prop:family\" label=\"prop:family\"></span>Put $d=2n$. There exist a primitive vector $h\\in\\Lambda$ with $q(h)>0$ and $q(h,e)=0$, a rational subspace $$P=\\langle e,f,v\\rangle_{\\mathbb Q}\\subset h^\\perp,\n \\qquad \\operatorname{sign}(q|_P)=(2,1),$$ and a projective semistable family $\\pi:\\mathcal Y\\to\\Delta$ with the following properties. Here $\\Delta$ is a sufficiently small disk in a smooth algebraic curve, and $\\Delta^*=\\Delta\\setminus\\{0\\}$.*\n\n1.  *The total space $\\mathcal Y$ is smooth, the central fiber is a reduced simple normal crossings divisor, and every $Y_t$, $t\\ne0$, is an irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifold in the marked deformation component of $X$. A line bundle $\\mathcal A$ on $\\mathcal Y$ restricts to an ample bundle on every $Y_t$ with $c_1(\\mathcal A|_{Y_t})=kh$ for a fixed positive integer $k$ under parallel transport. The family is the analytic restriction of an algebraic family.*\n\n\n\n\n\n\n2.  *The rational vectors can be chosen so that $$\\label{eq:family-hyperbolic-basis}\n     q(e)=q(f)=0,\\quad q(e,f)=1,\\quad\n     q(e,v)=q(f,v)=0,\\quad a:=q(v)>0.$$ On the universal cover of $\\Delta^*$ the marked Hodge line is spanned by the normalized vector $$\\label{eq:family-period}\n     p(\\tau)=f+\\tau v-\\frac a2\\tau^2e,\n     \\qquad \\operatorname{Im}\\tau>0.$$ The function $\\tau$ tends to the cusp represented by the original line $\\mathbb Qe$, and $$\\label{eq:family-period-identities}\n     q(p(\\tau))=0,\\qquad q(p(\\tau),e)=1,\\qquad\n     q(p(\\tau),\\overline{p(\\tau)})\n           =2a(\\operatorname{Im}\\tau)^2.$$ For a consistent convention for second-cohomology monodromy there is a rational $w>0$ such that $$\\label{eq:family-monodromy}\n     Me=e,\\qquad Mv=v-aw e,\\qquad\n     Mf=f+w v-\\frac{aw^2}{2}e,\n     \\qquad M|_{P^\\perp}=1.$$ The orthogonal complement in the last formula is taken in $\\Lambda_{\\mathbb Q}$. In particular, $$\\label{eq:family-monodromy-image}\n     (M-1)P=\\langle e,v\\rangle_{\\mathbb Q},\\quad\n     (M-1)^2f=-aw^2e\\ne0,\\quad (M-1)^3=0.$$ The same image statement holds if the monodromy convention uses $M^{-1}$.*\n\n\n\n3.  *There is a single-valued holomorphic relative symplectic form $\\sigma_t$ on the smooth family, uniquely normalized by $q([\\sigma_t],e)=1$. The forms $\\eta_t=\\sigma_t^n$ extend to a holomorphic section of the relative logarithmic top-form line bundle of the semistable family. With $s=-\\log|t|$, $$\\label{eq:family-volume-growth}\n     c s^d\\le \\int_{Y_t}|\\eta_t|^2\\le C s^d$$ for positive constants $c,C$. There is a point $o\\in Y_0$ where $d+1$ components meet and coordinates satisfying $$\\label{eq:family-maximal-residue}\n     t=z_0\\cdots z_d,\\qquad\n     \\eta=F(z)\\,\\frac{\\mathrm dz_1}{z_1}\\wedge\\cdots\\wedge\n                       \\frac{\\mathrm dz_d}{z_d},\\qquad F(o)\\ne0.$$*\n\n4.  *For any prescribed irrational number $\\lambda$, there is a path tending to $0$ in $\\Delta^*$, with a continuous choice of lift, along which $\\tau=\\lambda+iy$ and $y\\to+\\infty$.",
          "source_id": "PM-041.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "<span id=\"prop:family\" label=\"prop:family\"></span>Put $d=2n$. There exist a primitive vector $h\\in\\Lambda$ with $q(h)>0$ and $q(h,e)=0$, a rational subspace $$P=\\langle e,f,v\\rangle_{\\mathbb Q}\\subset h^\\perp,\n \\qquad \\operatorname{sign}(q|_P)=(2,1),$$ and a projective semistable family $\\pi:\\mathcal Y\\to\\Delta$ with the following properties. Here $\\Delta$ is a sufficiently small disk in a smooth algebraic curve, and $\\Delta^*=\\Delta\\setminus\\{0\\}$.*\n\n1.  *The total space $\\mathcal Y$ is smooth, the central fiber is a reduced simple normal crossings divisor, and every $Y_t$, $t\\ne0$, is an irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifold in the marked deformation component of $X$. A line bundle $\\mathcal A$ on $\\mathcal Y$ restricts to an ample bundle on every $Y_t$ with $c_1(\\mathcal A|_{Y_t})=kh$ for a fixed positive integer $k$ under parallel transport. The family is the analytic restriction of an algebraic family.*\n\n\n\n\n\n\n2.  *The rational vectors can be chosen so that $$\\label{eq:family-hyperbolic-basis}\n     q(e)=q(f)=0,\\quad q(e,f)=1,\\quad\n     q(e,v)=q(f,v)=0,\\quad a:=q(v)>0.$$ On the universal cover of $\\Delta^*$ the marked Hodge line is spanned by the normalized vector $$\\label{eq:family-period}\n     p(\\tau)=f+\\tau v-\\frac a2\\tau^2e,\n     \\qquad \\operatorname{Im}\\tau>0.$$ The function $\\tau$ tends to the cusp represented by the original line $\\mathbb Qe$, and $$\\label{eq:family-period-identities}\n     q(p(\\tau))=0,\\qquad q(p(\\tau),e)=1,\\qquad\n     q(p(\\tau),\\overline{p(\\tau)})\n           =2a(\\operatorname{Im}\\tau)^2.$$ For a consistent convention for second-cohomology monodromy there is a rational $w>0$ such that $$\\label{eq:family-monodromy}\n     Me=e,\\qquad Mv=v-aw e,\\qquad\n     Mf=f+w v-\\frac{aw^2}{2}e,\n     \\qquad M|_{P^\\perp}=1.$$ The orthogonal complement in the last formula is taken in $\\Lambda_{\\mathbb Q}$. In particular, $$\\label{eq:family-monodromy-image}\n     (M-1)P=\\langle e,v\\rangle_{\\mathbb Q},\\quad\n     (M-1)^2f=-aw^2e\\ne0,\\quad (M-1)^3=0.$$ The same image statement holds if the monodromy convention uses $M^{-1}$.*\n\n\n\n3.  *There is a single-valued holomorphic relative symplectic form $\\sigma_t$ on the smooth family, uniquely normalized by $q([\\sigma_t],e)=1$. The forms $\\eta_t=\\sigma_t^n$ extend to a holomorphic section of the relative logarithmic top-form line bundle of the semistable family. With $s=-\\log|t|$, $$\\label{eq:family-volume-growth}\n     c s^d\\le \\int_{Y_t}|\\eta_t|^2\\le C s^d$$ for positive constants $c,C$. There is a point $o\\in Y_0$ where $d+1$ components meet and coordinates satisfying $$\\label{eq:family-maximal-residue}\n     t=z_0\\cdots z_d,\\qquad\n     \\eta=F(z)\\,\\frac{\\mathrm dz_1}{z_1}\\wedge\\cdots\\wedge\n                       \\frac{\\mathrm dz_d}{z_d},\\qquad F(o)\\ne0.$$*\n\n4.  *For any prescribed irrational number $\\lambda$, there is a path tending to $0$ in $\\Delta^*$, with a continuous choice of lift, along which $\\tau=\\lambda+iy$ and $y\\to+\\infty$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-polarized-family-construction",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be a compact irreducible holomorphic symplectic Kähler manifold, fix its connected marked deformation component, and put $\\Lambda=H^2(X,\\mathbb Z)$ with its integral BBF form $q$. For each nonzero primitive $e\\in\\Lambda$ with $q(e)=0$, there are*\n\n1.  *a primitive $h\\in\\Lambda$ with $q(h)>0$ and $q(h,e)=0$, and a manifold in the chosen marked component on which $h$ is ample;*\n\n2.  *a smooth projective algebraic family over a smooth connected quasi-projective base $S$, whose fibers are irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifolds in that component, with a relative polarization of marked class $kh$ for one $k\\in\\mathbb Z_{>0}$;*\n\n3.  *a torsion-free finite-index subgroup $$\\Gamma\\subset G_h(\\mathbb Z)\n     :=\\{g\\in O(\\Lambda,q):gh=h,\\ gD_h=D_h\\},$$ where $D_h$ is the component of the polarized period domain containing the chosen ample period, such that the family monodromy is contained in $\\Gamma$ and the descended period map $$\\Phi:S\\longrightarrow Q_h:=\\Gamma\\backslash D_h$$ is algebraic. The quotient $Q_h$ is smooth and quasi-projective, $D_h\\to Q_h$ is a covering map with free $\\Gamma$-action, and a local marked lift of $\\Phi$ is submersive at some point of $S$.*\n\n*More generally, conclusions <span class=\"upright\">(2)</span> and <span class=\"upright\">(3)</span> hold for any prescribed primitive positive $h\\in\\Lambda$ which is ample on a manifold in the chosen marked component. The markings are obtained by deformation and parallel transport from $X$. No finite-index assertion about the image of the family monodromy inside $\\Gamma$ is part of this construction.",
        "source_id": "PM-041.2"
      },

The quotient $Q_h$ is smooth and quasi-projective, $D_h\\to Q_h$ is a covering map with free $\\Gamma$-action, and a local marked lift of $\\Phi$ is submersive at some point of $S$.*\n\n*More generally, conclusions <span class=\"upright\">(2)</span> and <span class=\"upright\">(3)</span> hold for any prescribed primitive positive $h\\in\\Lambda$ which is ample on a manifold in the chosen marked component.         "exact_quote": "For every sufficiently large $y$, the corresponding smooth fiber contains an embedded special Lagrangian real $d$-torus for its Ricci-flat metric in the polarization class. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Compact irreducible holomorphic symplectic Kähler manifold** ([source](#pm-h-2)): A compact irreducible holomorphic symplectic Kähler manifold is a simply connected compact Kähler manifold $X$ such that $H^0(X,\Omega_X^2)=\mathbb C\sigma$ for a holomorphic two-form $\sigma$ which is nondegenerate everywhere.
- **Beauville–Bogomolov–Fujiki form** ([source](#pm-h-2)): We write $q=q_X$ for the Beauville–Bogomolov–Fujiki quadratic form, and also for its associated symmetric bilinear form.
- **Kähler cone notation** ([source](#pm-h-2)): We denote the Kähler cone by $\mathcal K_X$.
- **Nef, isotropic, and semiample line bundles** ([source](#pm-h-2)): A line bundle $L$ is *nef* if $c_1(L)$ lies in the closure of the Kähler cone, *isotropic* if $q(c_1(L))=0$, and *semiample* if some positive tensor power is generated by its global holomorphic sections.
- **Smooth deformation type** ([source](#pm-h-2)): Here a deformation type means an equivalence class under smooth proper deformations through compact irreducible holomorphic symplectic Kähler manifolds.
- **Special Lagrangian torus conditions** ([source](#stmt-thm-torus)): Here special Lagrangian means that the Kähler form restricts to zero and that $\eta_t$ has constant phase on the oriented torus.
- **Torus-scale Ricci-flat metric and volume** ([source](#eq:torus-scaling)): The useful scale is $$g_t=s g_t^{(0)},\qquad \omega_t=s\omega_t^{(0)},\qquad \mu_t=\mathrm dV_t=\frac{\omega_t^d}{d!},\qquad \mu_t(Y_t)=c_a s^d, \quad c_a=\frac1{d!}\int_{Y_t}a_t^d>0. \label{eq:torus-scaling}$$
- **Logarithmic flat coordinate metric** ([source](#pm-h-7)): On its smooth fibers set $$x_i=\log\|z_i\|,\qquad y_i=\arg z_i\in\mathbb R/(2\pi\mathbb Z),\qquad \gamma=\sum_{i=1}^d(\mathrm dx_i^2+\mathrm dy_i^2),\qquad \omega_\gamma=\sum_{i=1}^d\mathrm dx_i\wedge\mathrm dy_i.$$
- **Interior logarithmic tube** ([source](#pm-h-7)): For a relatively compact subset $B\Subset\Sigma$, write $$\mathcal T_t(B)=\{x/s\in B,\quad y\in(\mathbb R/2\pi\mathbb Z)^d\}.$$
- **Centered maximal function** ([source](#eq:maximal-weak-type)): For a nonnegative integrable function $h$ on $Y_t$, let $$\mathcal M_t h(q)=\sup_{r>0} \frac1{\mu_t(B_{g_t}(q,r))} \int_{B_{g_t}(q,r)}h\,\mathrm dV_t$$ be the centered maximal function.
- **Limit logarithmic coordinate map** ([source](#pm-h-12)): Thus $$F=(u_1+i\vartheta_1,\ldots,u_d+i\vartheta_d):G\longrightarrow H \label{eq:limit-map}$$ is holomorphic.
- **Second-cohomology lattice and rationalization** ([source](#sec:family)): All markings take values in $$\Lambda=H^2(X,\mathbb Z),\qquad \Lambda_{\mathbb Q}=\Lambda\otimes_{\mathbb Z}\mathbb Q, \qquad r=\operatorname{rank}\Lambda,$$ with its integral Beauville–Bogomolov–Fujiki form $q$.
- **Positive fixed-class deformation locus** ([source](#pm-h-28)): Write $\mathcal C_I$ for the component of the positive cone containing the Kähler cone of $(N,I)$, and set $$\mathcal T^\circ_e =\{[I]\in\mathcal T^\circ(N): e\in H^{1,1}(N,I),\ e\in\overline{\mathcal C_I}\}.$$

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-finiteness-betti-threshold | the second Betti number | ≥ | $5$ | The threshold is used to obtain an isotropic vector for the finiteness deduction, not for the main semiampleness theorem. |
| q-scaled-total-volume | the total measure of the fiber | = | $c_a s^d$ | The polarization volume is independent of the nonzero parameter over the punctured disk. |
| q-mixed-intersection-upper-bound | the mixed volume integral over the tubular neighborhood | ≤ | $C_B s^d$ | The tube is fixed in rescaled radial coordinates; its bound is uniform near the puncture. |
| q-curvature-global-energy | the integral of the squared Riemann curvature tensor over the fiber | ≤ | $C s^{d-2}$ | The unscaled Chern–Weil integral is fixed along the smooth family. |
| q-projective-map-global-energy | the Dirichlet energy of the map over the fiber | ≤ | $C s^{d-1}$ | The embedding is fixed before degeneration and metric rescaling. |
| q-average-vanishing-energy | the parameterized mass quantity | ≤ | $C(s^{-2}+s^{-1})$ | The degeneration parameter approaches the puncture. |
| q-noncollapsed-volume-fraction | the measure of the exceptional set at the given parameter | ≥ | $\delta\mu_t(Y_t)$ | The positive fraction is uniform for sufficiently small nonzero parameters. |
| q-limit-singular-dimension | the Hausdorff dimension of the singular set | ≤ | $N-4$ | The bound precedes the later removal of all apparent singularities. |
| q-hyperbolic-basis-pairing | the bilinear form evaluated on the pair of elements | = | $1$ | The equality fixes the normalization of the rational hyperbolic basis. |
| q-normalized-period-pairing | the quadratic form evaluated on the curve and its complex conjugate | = | $2a(\operatorname{Im}\tau)^2$ | The imaginary part of the period parameter is positive. |
| q-monodromy-nilpotent-cube | the cubic power of the shifted parameter | = | $0$ | The formula includes the identity action on the orthogonal complement; finite base changes preserve the stated nilpotency. |
| q-family-volume-upper-growth | the integral of the squared norm of the form on the fiber | ≤ | $C s^d$ | The upper bound is uniform near the puncture. |
| q-family-volume-lower-growth | the integral of the squared norm of the form on the fiber | ≥ | $c s^d$ | The positive lower bound forces a maximal crossing with nonzero coefficient. |
| q-local-crossing-volume | the integral of the squared norm of the form over a coordinate chart | ≤ | $C(1+s^{k-1})$ | The growth depends on the number of central-fiber components meeting in that chart. |
| q-rotated-rational-class-square | the quadratic form evaluated at the element | ≤ | $0$ | The inequality does not assert that every rational Picard class is isotropic. |
| q-lagrangian-base-dimension | the complex dimension of the base manifold | = | $n$ | The base dimension is half the complex dimension of the total space. |

There is a point $o\\in Y_0$ where $d+1$ components meet and coordinates satisfying $$\\label{eq:family-maximal-residue}\n     t=z_0\\cdots z_d,\\qquad\n     \\eta=F(z)\\,\\frac{\\mathrm dz_1}{z_1}\\wedge\\cdots\\wedge\n                       \\frac{\\mathrm dz_d}{z_d},\\qquad F(o)\\ne0.$$",

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-mixed-intersections** [claim](#stmt-lem-mixed-estimates) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-mixed-estimates): Resolve the rational coordinate maps on a fixed projective model, average their pulled-back positive forms, and compare fixed cohomological intersections with logarithmic forms. -> Mixed-intersection bounds and integrated trace bounds in both directions.
- **step-noncollapsed-centers** [claim](#stmt-lem-many-noncollapsed-centers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-many-noncollapsed-centers): Select disjoint coordinate boxes with controlled trace, average segment lengths, and use volume comparison to obtain uniformly noncollapsed intrinsic balls. -> A positive-volume set of noncollapsed centers.
- **step-harmonic-map-localization** [claim](#stmt-lem-small-energy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-small-energy): Use stationary harmonic-map monotonicity, derivative blow-up selection, and the Bochner mean-value estimate to exclude nonvanishing derivatives when local energy vanishes. -> Smooth subsequential constant limits and localization of regular regions in the crossing chart.
- **step-capacity-and-gradient-bounds** [claim](#stmt-lem-capacity-extension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-capacity-extension): Use small-capacity cutoffs and truncation to extend the coordinate functions weakly harmonically, then apply harmonic gradient estimates and the positive Jacobian to bound coordinate inverse derivatives. -> Global weak harmonic extensions and uniform derivative bounds.
- **step-regular-distance** [claim](#stmt-lem-regular-paths) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-regular-paths): Apply the segment inequality to weighted covers of the singular set and approximate ambient minimizing distances by paths avoiding that set. -> Equality of regular intrinsic and ambient distances, and a Lipschitz extension of the coordinate map.
- **step-full-cylinder** [claim](#stmt-lem-full-cylinder) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-full-cylinder): Lift polygonal paths outside the singular image by the local inverse, continue lifts using completeness, and extend the inverse by density. -> A bi-Lipschitz homeomorphism between the complete limit and the full cylinder.
- **step-smooth-actual-coordinates** [claim](#stmt-lem-actual-log-convergence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-actual-log-convergence): Remove the apparent metric singularities with bounded holomorphic coframes, prove translation invariance, and identify smooth local inverses with the actual logarithmic chart. -> Smooth convergence of the metric, Kähler form, and normalized volume form on fixed cylinder tubes with the original angular marking.
- **step-special-lagrangian-persistence** [claim](#stmt-thm-torus) / [proof](#pm-h-15): Use exactness of the Kähler form, normalize the volume-form phase, invert the special Lagrangian deformation operator on the harmonic-orthogonal space, and apply the inverse function theorem. -> Embedded smooth special Lagrangian tori in the coordinate isotopy class for every sufficiently small nonzero parameter.
- **step-polarized-family-and-quotient** [claim](#stmt-lem-polarized-family-construction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-polarized-family-construction): Choose an orthogonal positive integral polarization, realize generic polarized periods, construct a Hilbert family, and pass to a torsion-free arithmetic quotient with free action. -> An algebraic polarized family with a submersive marked period lift and a free period quotient.
- **step-prescribed-cusp-degeneration** [claim](#stmt-prop-polarized-family) / [proof](#pm-h-22): Approximate a period plane by a rational ternary subspace retaining the supplied vector, reach its labelled cusp through an algebraic curve, identify full-lattice monodromy by freeness, and perform semistable reduction. Period growth extends the logarithmic form and forces a nonzero maximal residue. -> The prescribed-cusp semistable family with exact periods, monodromy, volume growth, and nonzero maximal residue.
- **step-family-torus** [claim](#stmt-cor-family-torus) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-family-torus): Apply the degeneration torus theorem to the prescribed-cusp family along its lifted irrational vertical period ray. -> A torus in the required coordinate isotopy class on a fiber carrying the exact marked monodromy data.
- **step-cohomological-rotation** [claim](#stmt-lem-rotation-criterion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rotation-criterion): Eliminate terms involving the cohomologically trivial Kähler restriction from the special Lagrangian period and force equality in Wirtinger’s inequality. Use the Albanese map to identify the resulting complex torus. -> A holomorphic Lagrangian complex torus after hyperkähler rotation.
- **step-external-nonprojective-fibration** [claim](#stmt-thm-nonprojective-torus) / [proof](#pm-h-27): Invoke the explicitly stated nonprojective-torus fibration theorem; the supplied paper treats it as an external input rather than reproving it. -> A holomorphic Lagrangian fibration with normal projective base and a nontrivial semiample pullback bundle.
- **step-external-fixed-class-propagation** [claim](#stmt-thm-fixed-class) / [proof](#pm-h-28): Invoke the explicitly stated fixed-class deformation theorem on the connected positive-sign locus. -> Equality of the nef and semiample loci for the specified class.
- **step-return-and-fiber-geometry** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#pm-h-29): Use coordinate-torus monodromy invariance to satisfy the rotation criterion, and use irrationality to isolate the rational isotropic ray. The external fibration yields semiampleness of that prescribed ray; fixed-class propagation returns it to the original manifold. A generated power, Stein factorization, Fujiki’s relation, and equidimensionality establish the fiber description. -> Semiampleness on the original manifold and the stated equidimensional Lagrangian fibration.
- **step-projective-base-specialization** [claim](#stmt-cor-projective-base) / [proof](#subsec:projective-base): Exhibit the map as projective using a closed graph embedding and apply the stated companion base theorem and its divisorial multiplicity conclusion. -> A complex projective-space base and exclusion of the specified multiple-divisor pullbacks.
- **step-smooth-type-finiteness** [claim](#stmt-cor-finiteness) / [proof](#sec:finiteness): Use Meyer’s theorem to supply a primitive isotropic vector, realize a generic period with no other integral Picard direction, choose its nef sign, and apply semiampleness and the stated smooth boundedness consequence. -> Finiteness of smooth deformation types in the stated fixed dimension and Betti-number regime.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-prescribed-bundle-semiampleness**: The given nonzero nef isotropic line bundle is semiample on the original manifold and defines a connected-fiber holomorphic Lagrangian fibration over a normal projective base.
- **invariant-coordinate-isotopy-torus**: The degeneration supplies a smooth embedded special Lagrangian torus in the angular coordinate isotopy class on every sufficiently small nonzero fiber.
- **invariant-full-smooth-cylinder**: The limit is the full smooth flat cylinder, and convergence on its tubes occurs in the actual logarithmic coordinates with the original angular marking.
- **invariant-labelled-monodromy**: The degeneration retains the prescribed isotropic lattice vector, and its monodromy image contains the classes needed for cohomological rotation.
- **invariant-cohomological-rotation**: A compact special Lagrangian submanifold with vanishing restricted Kähler cohomology class becomes holomorphically Lagrangian after hyperkähler rotation; a torus remains a complex torus.
- **invariant-fixed-class-return**: Nonemptiness of the semiample locus makes the nef and semiample loci coincide for the specified primitive isotropic class in the positive-sign deformation locus.
- **invariant-deformation-finiteness-scope**: Smooth deformation finiteness is asserted in fixed complex dimension under the stated second-Betti-number restriction.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-numerical-sections-shortcut**: Treat isotropy and numerical dimension alone as a procedure for producing sections and eliminating the base locus. -> The numerical intersection condition does not itself construct global sections or establish base-point freedom.
- **anti-arbitrary-crossing-torus**: Apply the torus existence theorem to an arbitrary singular family or a crossing with vanishing logarithmic coefficient. -> The required cylinder-scale volume control and nonzero limiting volume form are unavailable.
- **anti-regular-set-only-convergence**: Perturb an entire angular torus using flatness and convergence only on the initially known regular set. -> An apparent singular set can obstruct convergence over the whole torus.
- **anti-unlabelled-torus-rotation**: Replace the constructed torus by any special Lagrangian torus and assume the required restricted Kähler class vanishes. -> Without the coordinate isotopy class, the monodromy restriction identity used in rotation has not been established.
- **anti-rational-ray-substitution**: Replace the prescribed irrational period ray by a rational one while claiming the same uniqueness of the rational isotropic class. -> The argument forcing the rational pairings to vanish depends on irrationality and no longer establishes the specified isotropic ray.
- **anti-unspecified-semiample-deformation**: Return semiampleness to the original bundle from a deformation carrying an unrelated semiample isotropic class. -> Existence of another semiample class does not identify a semiample point for the prescribed primitive class.
- **anti-projective-base-without-projectivity**: Conclude that the base is complex projective space for every compact Kähler total space covered by the main theorem. -> The main theorem supplies a normal projective base, while its identification with projective space uses the additional projectivity hypothesis on the total space.
- **anti-unrestricted-finiteness**: Apply the deformation-finiteness corollary after dropping its second-Betti-number restriction. -> The proof no longer obtains a supplied isotropic class from the stated arithmetic theorem.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The central conclusions concern existence and deformation of compact complex manifolds, line bundles, and smooth special Lagrangian tori, rather than a finite constructive input-output problem. The torus proof explicitly states: “The inverse function theorem therefore produces $\alpha_t\to0$ in $C^{k+1,\beta}$ with $\mathcal P_t(\alpha_t)=0$ for every sufficiently late member of the subsequence.” It then passes from an arbitrary sequence to all sufficiently small nonzero parameters by contradiction. The return to the original manifold uses the fixed-class deformation implication. These analytic and deformation existence steps supply neither a finite representation of admissible inputs nor an effective bound for constructing sections or the torus. Finite checks of the displayed monodromy identities would cover only algebraic ingredients and would not certify these existence conclusions.

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Semiampleness supplies an existential geometric map from a manifold under restrictive geometric hypotheses. The paper does not provide finite input representations or an executable procedure for constructing that map. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The special Lagrangian torus is obtained in a degenerating family equipped with Ricci-flat metrics. Its geometric existence and isotopy do not specify a mesh, simulation update rule, or discretisation error guarantee. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Smooth convergence in the actual logarithmic coordinates identifies the limiting geometric tensors along a selected subsequence. It supplies no computable stopping criterion for deciding when a given nearby fiber is sufficiently close to that limit. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The torus perturbation uses an inverse function theorem on spaces of differential forms. This analytic existence argument does not provide a finite algorithm, an effective perturbation threshold, or a complexity bound. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The lattice, marked periods, and arithmetic quotient organise cohomological information. Their mathematical definitions do not specify storage layouts, query operations, or finite representations of the deformation spaces. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The energy estimates describe curvature and projective-map energy under rescaling of a Ricci-flat metric. They measure geometric degeneration rather than arithmetic workloads, memory traffic, or acceleration opportunities. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Freeness of the arithmetic action identifies family monodromy from equal lifted periods. This geometric uniqueness argument supplies neither a cryptographic hardness assumption nor a security protocol. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Vanishing energy implies local derivative decay for smooth harmonic maps on smoothly converging domains. The assumptions concern an analytic equation and geometric convergence, not training losses or learned predictors. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Mixed-intersection estimates bound integrals of geometric forms and metric traces on interior coordinate tubes. They do not formulate a finite optimisation problem or provide a method for selecting optimal feasible solutions. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Path lifting through a covering space transports a marking towards a specified cusp. The resulting consistency of period lifts is a topological property, with no message-passing, failure-recovery, or distributed coordination construction. |
| other (Complex geometry and geometric analysis) | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper supplies mathematical constructions of degenerations, special Lagrangian tori, and fibrations. These can inform geometric research, but the compactness and deformation arguments do not yield a finite engineering contract for producing the objects. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-ambient-geometric-category** (hypothesis): The semiampleness assertion concerns compact irreducible holomorphic symplectic Kähler manifolds. It does not establish the same conclusion on arbitrary complex or symplectic manifolds. Blocked use: ['Apply the semiampleness theorem without verifying the stated ambient geometric category.']
- **limit-nonzero-prescribed-class** (hypothesis): The first Chern class must be nonzero. The asserted base dimension and fiber description cannot be assigned to a bundle with vanishing first Chern class by this theorem. Blocked use: ['Use the asserted fibration description for a bundle with vanishing first Chern class.']
- **limit-nefness-on-original-manifold** (hypothesis): The prescribed first Chern class must lie in the closure of the Kähler cone on the original manifold. Isotropy alone does not satisfy the theorem's assumptions. Blocked use: ['Infer semiampleness on the original manifold from isotropy without nefness.']
- **limit-isotropic-square** (hypothesis): Vanishing of the Beauville–Bogomolov–Fujiki square is required. The conclusion about half-dimensional Lagrangian fibers is not asserted for classes of positive or negative square. Blocked use: ['Extend the stated fiber geometry to a line bundle whose first Chern class is not isotropic.']
- **limit-generated-power-effectivity** (unknown): An effective bound for a globally generated tensor power, a finite procedure for finding its sections, and computational complexity guarantees are ABSENT. The existence of such a power is a geometric conclusion. Blocked use: ['Treat semiampleness as a supplied terminating algorithm for computing sections.', 'Assign an effective tensor-power bound from the existential statement.']
- **limit-semistable-polarized-family** (hypothesis): The torus construction assumes an algebraic projective family with smooth total space, reduced simple normal crossings central fiber, smooth connected fibers with trivial canonical bundle, and an extending polarizing line bundle. It does not apply to arbitrary singular families. Blocked use: ['Invoke torus existence for a degeneration lacking the stated semistable and polarization assumptions.', 'Require relative ampleness at the central fiber as though it were an assumption of the torus theorem.']
- **limit-maximal-growth-and-residue** (hypothesis): The logarithmic volume growth and the nonzero coefficient at a maximal crossing are substantive torus hypotheses. A crossing with vanishing coefficient cannot supply the same lower-growth argument. Blocked use: ['Construct the claimed torus from a crossing with vanishing logarithmic coefficient.', 'Drop the lower logarithmic volume bound while invoking the maximal-residue deduction.']
- **limit-interior-tube-and-integrated-traces** (formal_scope): The mixed-intersection and trace bounds concern fixed relatively compact radial regions inside the logarithmic simplex. They are integrated estimates and do not themselves give pointwise comparison of the metrics throughout a tube or control at its boundary. Blocked use: ['Replace an integrated trace bound by uniform pointwise metric bounds.', 'Move the radial region to the boundary without establishing new estimates.']
- **limit-noncollapse-and-center-selection** (formal_scope): The noncollapse assertion holds on a selected positive-volume set of centers, and the simultaneous all-radius energy bounds require a further center selection. They do not assert uniform noncollapse or vanishing local energy at every point of the fiber. Blocked use: ['Use an arbitrary center as though it satisfied the selected-center estimates.', 'Infer uniform noncollapse throughout the whole degenerating manifold.']
- **limit-harmonic-map-local-regime** (hypothesis): The small-energy conclusion requires smooth harmonic maps, smoothly converging domain metrics, a fixed compact target, and energy vanishing on every relatively compact subdomain. Its derivative conclusion is local on interior compact sets. Blocked use: ['Infer derivative decay from small energy for maps that are not harmonic.', 'Extend the interior conclusion to boundary points without additional estimates.', 'Replace smooth domain convergence by sampled metric agreement.']
- **limit-singular-set-removal** (formal_scope): Flatness of the initial regular set does not establish smooth convergence over an entire angular torus. Weak harmonic extension, distance comparison, full-cylinder completion, and removal of apparent singularities are required before the torus perturbation. Blocked use: ['Skip singular-set removal when applying persistence to a whole torus.', 'Identify a flat regular part with the full smooth metric completion without proving the extension steps.']
- **limit-subsequence-and-analytic-persistence** (nonconstructive): Actual-coordinate convergence is stated along the selected subsequence. The torus theorem obtains its conclusion for all sufficiently small nonzero parameters by applying the construction to arbitrary sequences and excluding counterexamples. The argument supplies no effective degeneration threshold or finite perturbation solver. Blocked use: ['Claim smooth convergence of the full family from the selected-subsequence convergence statement.', 'Extract a computable stopping criterion from the inverse function theorem.', 'Treat finite numerical agreement as certification of torus existence.']
- **limit-labelled-class-and-monodromy** (formal_scope): The degeneration retains the prescribed class as a labelled lattice vector; it need not retain its original Hodge type during construction. The arithmetic subgroup has finite index, but no finite-index assertion is made for the family-monodromy image. Blocked use: ['Replace the prescribed class by an unrelated isotropic vector.', 'Assume the class stays of Hodge type throughout the construction.', 'Infer finite index of the family-monodromy image from finite index of the containing arithmetic subgroup.']
- **limit-coordinate-isotopy-for-rotation** (hypothesis): The rotation criterion requires vanishing of the restricted Kähler cohomology class in addition to the special Lagrangian assumptions. The constructed torus's coordinate isotopy class establishes this vanishing through monodromy. The complex-torus conclusion additionally requires the stated torus topology. Blocked use: ['Rotate an arbitrary special Lagrangian torus without checking the restricted cohomology class.', 'Discard angular parametrization and still claim the monodromy restriction identity.', 'Infer complex-torus topology for an arbitrary rotated special Lagrangian submanifold.']
- **limit-irrational-period-and-isotropic-ray** (hypothesis): Irrationality of the selected real period parameter forces the rational pairings to vanish. The resulting uniqueness concerns rational isotropic classes; the rational Picard space may still contain classes of negative square. Blocked use: ['Substitute a rational period parameter while retaining the same uniqueness argument.', 'Assert that every rational Picard class is isotropic.']
- **limit-external-fibration-and-fixed-class-return** (hypothesis): The invoked torus-to-fibration theorem assumes a nonprojective compact irreducible hyperkähler manifold containing a holomorphic Lagrangian complex torus. Returning semiampleness requires a semiample point for the same primitive class in the same positive-sign marked deformation locus, and nefness at the original point. Blocked use: ['Apply the nonprojective-torus theorem to a manifold without its stated assumptions.', 'Transfer semiampleness from an unrelated class or a differently signed deformation locus.', 'Use a semiample deformation to remove the original nefness hypothesis.']
- **limit-projective-space-base-specialization** (hypothesis): Identification of the base with complex projective space and exclusion of the specified divisorial multiplicities use additional projectivity of the total space. These conclusions are not stated for every compact Kähler total space in the main theorem. Blocked use: ['Identify the base with projective space without establishing projectivity of the total space.', 'Extend the divisor assertion to unmentioned notions of multiple fibers.']
- **limit-smooth-finiteness-betti-regime** (formal_scope): Smooth deformation finiteness retains the second-Betti-number hypothesis used to obtain an isotropic vector through Meyer's theorem. This restriction is not imposed on the main theorem, which starts with a supplied isotropic class. The finiteness conclusion supplies no explicit count or enumeration of deformation types. Let $\\eta_t$ be nowhere vanishing holomorphic $d$-forms which extend as a section of the relative logarithmic canonical line bundle. Put $s=-\\log|t|$, and suppose that, for constants $c,C>0$, $$c s^d\\le I_t:=\\int_{Y_t}|\\eta_t|^2\\le C s^d,\n \\qquad 0<|t|\\ll1.\n \\label{eq:log-volume-growth}$$ Suppose, moreover, that at some point $o\\in\\mathcal Y_0$ exactly $d+1$ components meet and that the logarithmic coefficient of $\\eta$ at $o$ is nonzero. Thus in a coordinate neighborhood of $o$, $$z_0z_1\\cdots z_d=t,\n \\qquad\n \\eta_t=f(z)\\,\\frac{\\mathrm dz_1}{z_1}\\wedge\\cdots\\wedge\n                         \\frac{\\mathrm dz_d}{z_d},\n \\qquad f(o)\\ne0.\n \\label{eq:crossing-chart}$$ Equip $Y_t$ with the Ricci-flat Kähler metric in $a_t$. For every sufficiently small nonzero $t$, the fiber $Y_t$ contains a smooth embedded special Lagrangian real $d$-torus isotopic in $Y_t$ to an angular coordinate torus in <a href=\"#eq:crossing-chart\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:crossing-chart\">[eq:crossing-chart]</a>. Here special Lagrangian means that the Kähler form restricts to zero and that $\\eta_t$ has constant phase on the oriented torus.",

The second identity follows by contraction in an orthonormal Lagrangian frame. This operator is an isomorphism $\\mathcal V\\to\\mathcal W$. To see this, rewrite its second equation as $\\delta\\alpha=f$, with $f$ of mean zero. For an exact two-form $\\xi$, the Hodge Green operator $\\mathcal G$ gives $$\\alpha=\\delta\\mathcal G\\xi+\\mathrm d\\mathcal Gf.$$ The Hodge identities give $\\mathrm d\\alpha=\\xi$ and $\\delta\\alpha=f$. <a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the strong hyperkähler SYZ conjecture: every holomorphic line bundle with nonzero nef isotropic first Chern class on a compact irreducible holomorphic symplectic Kähler manifold is semiample.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>

<a id="pm-h-2"></a>
### Introduction

A compact irreducible holomorphic symplectic Kähler manifold is a simply connected compact Kähler manifold $X$ such that $H^0(X,\Omega_X^2)=\mathbb C\sigma$ for a holomorphic two-form $\sigma$ which is nondegenerate everywhere. Its complex dimension is even, say $2n$. We write $q=q_X$ for the Beauville–Bogomolov–Fujiki quadratic form, and also for its associated symmetric bilinear form. We denote the Kähler cone by $\mathcal K_X$. A line bundle $L$ is *nef* if $c_1(L)$ lies in the closure of the Kähler cone, *isotropic* if $q(c_1(L))=0$, and *semiample* if some positive tensor power is generated by its global holomorphic sections.

A nonzero nef isotropic class has numerical dimension $n$, by Fujiki’s relation: its powers have positive intersection with a Kähler class through degree $n$, and zero intersection in higher degrees. The strong hyperkähler SYZ conjecture asks whether every line bundle with such a class is semiample (Verbitsky 2010, Conjecture 1.7) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[VerbitskySYZ](#ref-VerbitskySYZ)]. This would turn a class on the boundary of the Kähler cone into a holomorphic map with half-dimensional fibers. The numerical condition by itself supplies neither sections nor control of their base locus.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Let $X$ be a compact irreducible holomorphic symplectic Kähler manifold of complex dimension $2n$, where $n\ge1$. If a holomorphic line bundle $L$ satisfies $$c_1(L)\ne0,\qquad c_1(L)\in\overline{\mathcal K_X},
                  \qquad q_X(c_1(L))=0,$$ then $L$ is semiample.*

*More precisely, there exist a positive integer $m$, a normal projective variety $B$, an ample line bundle $A$ on $B$, and a surjective holomorphic map with connected fibers $$f:X\longrightarrow B,\qquad L^m\simeq f^*A,$$ such that $\dim_{\mathbb C}B=n$. Every irreducible component of every fiber has complex dimension $n$, and $\sigma$ vanishes on its smooth locus.*

</div>

Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> resolves the strong hyperkähler SYZ conjecture positively. The conclusion holds on the given manifold $X$: a positive tensor power of $L$ has no base points anywhere. No restriction on the dimension, deformation type, or second Betti number is imposed.

There is also a consequence for the number of possible smooth manifolds. Here a deformation type means an equivalence class under smooth proper deformations through compact irreducible holomorphic symplectic Kähler manifolds.

<a id="stmt-cor-finiteness"></a>
<div id="cor:finiteness" class="corollary">

**Corollary 2**. *For each integer $n\ge1$, there are only finitely many smooth deformation types of compact irreducible holomorphic symplectic Kähler manifolds of complex dimension $2n$ with $b_2\ge5$.*

</div>

This follows by combining Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> with the boundedness of Lagrangian-fibered symplectic varieties proved by Engel–Filipazzi–Greer–Mauri–Svaldi (Engel et al. 2025) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[EFGMS](#ref-EFGMS)]. Section <a href="#sec:finiteness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:finiteness">6</a> gives the deduction in the smooth category, including the role of the hypothesis $b_2\ge5$.

<a id="stmt-cor-projective-base"></a>
<div id="cor:projective-base" class="corollary">

**Corollary 3** (Projective case). *In Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, assume additionally that $X$ is projective, and let $f:X\to B$ be the associated connected-fiber map furnished there. Then $B\simeq\mathbb P^n_{\mathbb C}$. Moreover, there do not exist a prime divisor $D\subset B$, an effective divisor $E$ on $X$, and an integer $r>1$ such that $f^*D=rE$.*

</div>

This is a consequence of the projective-space base theorem in the companion (OpenAI 2026, Theorem 1.1 and the following paragraph on codimension-one multiple fibers) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[ProjectiveSpaceBases2026](#ref-ProjectiveSpaceBases2026)]. Its application is given after the proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, in Section <a href="#subsec:projective-base" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="subsec:projective-base">5.6</a>.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Context

The fibration picture is related to the special Lagrangian formulation of mirror symmetry proposed by Strominger–Yau–Zaslow (Strominger, Yau, and Zaslow 1996) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[SYZ](#ref-SYZ)]. In the hyperkähler setting, rotation of complex structure relates special Lagrangian and holomorphic Lagrangian geometry; the original paper already discusses this relation for K3 surfaces (Strominger, Yau, and Zaslow 1996, sec. 4.1) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[SYZ](#ref-SYZ)]. Early explicit nef-isotropic formulations appear in Hassett–Tschinkel (Hassett and Tschinkel 2001, Remark 3.12) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[HassettTschinkel](#ref-HassettTschinkel)] and Sawon (Sawon 2003, Conjecture 4.1) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Sawon](#ref-Sawon)]. The former asks for semiampleness, while the latter relates the existence of a nef square-zero divisor to the existence of an abelian fibration. Matsushita established the half-dimensional and Lagrangian character of fibrations from projective irreducible symplectic manifolds (Matsushita 1999, 2001) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Matsushita1999](#ref-Matsushita1999), [MatsushitaAddendum](#ref-MatsushitaAddendum)]; his equidimensionality theorem also controls every component of every fiber (Matsushita 2000, Theorem 1) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[MatsushitaEquidimensional](#ref-MatsushitaEquidimensional)]. Thus, once semiampleness is known, the geometry of the resulting map fits the expected holomorphic symplectic fibration picture.

Analytic positivity gives another source of progress. Verbitsky proves that a nontrivial nef isotropic bundle with a smooth semipositive Hermitian metric has a nonzero section in some positive power (Verbitsky 2010, Theorem 1.9) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[VerbitskySYZ](#ref-VerbitskySYZ)]. Höring–Lazić–Lehn obtain nonvanishing or coisotropic-locus alternatives for nef isotropic classes. In complex dimension four, they prove semiampleness if the class contains a closed positive current whose Lelong numbers all vanish (Höring, Lazić, and Lehn 2025, Theorem D and Corollary E) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[HoringLazicLehn](#ref-HoringLazicLehn)]. These results show how additional analytic positivity or geometric information can turn the numerical condition into sections.

The deformation approach has been particularly effective. Soldatenkov and Verbitsky show that a fixed isotropic class is semiample at every nef point of its deformation component as soon as it is semiample at one point (Soldatenkov and Verbitsky 2025, Theorem 3.7(v)) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[SV](#ref-SV)]. For the known deformation types, earlier constructions and birational results were obtained by Bayer–Macrì (Bayer and Macrì 2014, Theorem 1.5) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[BayerMacri](#ref-BayerMacri)] and Markman (Markman 2014, Theorem 1.3 and Remark 1.8) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[MarkmanFibrations](#ref-MarkmanFibrations)] for $\mathrm{K3}^{[n]}$-type, by Yoshioka (Yoshioka 2016, Proposition 3.38) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[Yoshioka](#ref-Yoshioka)] for generalized Kummer type, and by Mongardi–Rapagnetta (Mongardi and Rapagnetta 2021, Theorem 7.2) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[MongardiRapagnetta](#ref-MongardiRapagnetta)] and Mongardi–Onorati (Mongardi and Onorati 2022, Theorem 2.2) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[MongardiOnorati](#ref-MongardiOnorati)] for the two O’Grady types. These works combine moduli spaces of sheaves and stability conditions with monodromy and deformation methods. Their conclusions include fibrations on particular moduli spaces, results on the given manifold under nefness, and fibrations on birational models. Soldatenkov–Verbitsky’s deformation theorem implies the strong conjecture for all known hyperkähler deformation classes (Soldatenkov and Verbitsky 2025, sec. 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[SV](#ref-SV)]. This theorem treats compact Kähler manifolds and preserves the specified class; those two features are essential to our return from a nonprojective rotation to the original pair $(X,L)$.

The task left by this deformation theorem is to produce one semiample deformation of the *prescribed* class. We do so by constructing a torus and rotating the complex structure. The geometric input after rotation is the theorem of Greb–Lehn–Rollenske: a nonprojective hyperkähler manifold containing a holomorphic Lagrangian torus has a holomorphic Lagrangian fibration (Greb, Lehn, and Rollenske 2013, Theorem 4.1) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[GLR](#ref-GLR)].

The degeneration construction has a period-theoretic antecedent in Todorov’s work on maximally unipotent degeneration and vanishing torus cycles. His arithmetic-cusp construction relates isotropic classes to degenerations when $b_2\ge5$ (Todorov 2003, sec. 4) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[Todorov](#ref-Todorov)]. Here the given class is retained as a labelled vector in the full second-cohomology lattice through family realization and semistable reduction. Starting with the supplied isotropic vector imposes no additional lower bound on the second Betti number.

On the analytic side, Zhang develops the persistence of special Lagrangian tori in Calabi–Yau structures close to a flat cylinder (Zhang 2017, sec. 4) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[Zhang](#ref-Zhang)], using McLean’s deformation operator (McLean 1998) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[McLean](#ref-McLean)]. Li’s Fermat-family theorem gives special Lagrangian torus fibrations on generic regions whose relative volumes tend to one along a subsequence (Li 2022a, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[LiFermat](#ref-LiFermat)] <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[LiSurvey](#ref-LiSurvey)]; (Li 2022b) surveys the metric SYZ framework. For polarized algebraic maximal degenerations of compact Calabi–Yau manifolds, Blum–Liu’s valuative independence theorem (Blum and Liu 2026, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[BlumLiu2026](#ref-BlumLiu2026)] and Li’s comparison theorems (Li 2026, Theorems 1.4 and 1.8) <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[Li2026](#ref-Li2026)] give special Lagrangian torus fibrations on open sets whose normalized Calabi–Yau volumes are arbitrarily close to one near the degeneration. The semiampleness argument needs a local torus together with its coordinate isotopy class, which connects its geometry to the prescribed monodromy. We obtain that torus by proving convergence to a full smooth flat cylinder in the actual logarithmic coordinates; the final perturbation uses the Zhang–McLean method.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### The proof in four steps

Write $c_1(L)=m_0e$, where $m_0>0$ and $e$ is primitive integral. The proof produces a semiample deformation of $e$ and then returns to $X$.

1.  *Retain the prescribed class in monodromy.* Keep $e$ as a labelled integral cohomology class; it need not remain of type $(1,1)$ during this construction. Choose a polarization on a deformation of $X$ whose marked class $h$ is perpendicular to $e$. A rational three-dimensional period subspace containing $e$ gives an arithmetic curve with a cusp labelled by $\mathbb Qe$. We realize that curve by an algebraic family and track its full second-cohomology monodromy through semistable reduction (Proposition <a href="#prop:family" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:family">[prop:family]</a>).

2.  *Construct a torus in a known isotopy class.* For a Calabi–Yau degeneration of complex dimension $d$, with $s=-\log|t|$, multiply the polarized Ricci-flat metric by $s$. Intersection numbers control both traces of this metric relative to logarithmic coordinates. Together with the curvature and projective-map energy estimates, they select noncollapsed centers and produce a metric limit with flat regular part. We identify the full limit with a smooth flat cylinder, including its apparent singularities. In the actual logarithmic coordinates, convergence is smooth on a fixed tube containing an entire angular torus (Lemma <a href="#lem:actual-log-convergence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:actual-log-convergence">11</a>). A small special Lagrangian perturbation retains its coordinate isotopy class (Theorem <a href="#thm:torus" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:torus">4</a>). Here special Lagrangian means that the Kähler form vanishes on the torus and the holomorphic volume form has constant phase there.

3.  *Rotate using cohomology.* For a suitable fiber $Y$, write its hyperkähler triple as $(I,J,K)$, with $I$ its original complex structure. The coordinate torus is parameterized consistently around a loop of the family. Its restriction map therefore kills the image of $M-1$, where $M$ is monodromy on $H^2(Y,\mathbb R)$. The chosen periods put $[\omega_K]$ in that image. A cohomological rotation criterion (Lemma <a href="#lem:rotation-criterion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:rotation-criterion">15</a>) turns the special Lagrangian torus into a holomorphic Lagrangian torus for $J$. The rotated periods make $Y'=(Y,J)$ nonprojective and force every rational isotropic $(1,1)$ class to lie on $\mathbb Qe$. Thus the Greb–Lehn–Rollenske fibration makes $e$ semiample on $Y'$.

4.  *Return to the given manifold.* The markings and the positive sign of $e$ place $X$ and $Y'$ in the same fixed-class deformation locus. The Soldatenkov–Verbitsky theorem then gives semiampleness on $X$ itself. Fujiki’s formula and Matsushita’s equidimensionality theorem give the stated fiber properties.

The cylinder argument extends harmonic functions across the possible singular set to obtain a global derivative bound. Path lifting identifies the metric completion, and bounded holomorphic coframes remove its apparent singularities. In the rotation criterion, cohomological vanishing of one Kähler form suffices: the special Lagrangian period and equality in Wirtinger’s inequality supply the pointwise conclusion.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Organization and conventions

Sections <a href="#sec:metric-estimates" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:metric-estimates">2</a> and <a href="#sec:completion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:completion">3</a> prove the torus theorem independently of hyperkähler period theory. Section <a href="#sec:family" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:family">4</a> constructs the prescribed degeneration; Lemma <a href="#lem:polarized-family-construction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:polarized-family-construction">13</a> separates the underlying polarized family and free period quotient from the cusp calculation. Section <a href="#sec:rotation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:rotation">5</a> rotates the torus and proves Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>; Section <a href="#sec:finiteness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:finiteness">6</a> deduces Corollary <a href="#cor:finiteness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:finiteness">2</a>.

Unspecified cohomology coefficients are real. We normalize $q$ integrally; its signature is $(3,b_2-3)$, and the same symbol denotes its associated symmetric bilinear form. Fujiki’s relation is <a id="eq:fujiki"></a>$$\label{eq:fujiki}
        \int_X a^{2n}=c_Xq(a)^n,\qquad c_X>0.$$ The external inputs are stated where used: the Calabi–Yau theorem, hyperkähler period and Kähler-cone theory, arithmetic period-map algebraicity, semistable reduction, noncollapsed Einstein-limit and harmonic-function theory, and the fibration, deformation, and boundedness theorems cited above.

<a id="sec:metric-estimates"></a>
### Metric estimates at the torus scale

This section and the next construct a special Lagrangian torus near a maximal crossing of a polarized semistable degeneration. The coordinate isotopy class is essential: an angular coordinate torus is parameterized consistently around the puncture, so restriction to it is invariant under monodromy. Section <a href="#sec:rotation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:rotation">5</a> uses this identity in degree two. The construction applies to Calabi–Yau manifolds independently of the hyperkähler setting. We use $d$ for the complex dimension and $N=2d$ for the real dimension.

<a id="stmt-thm-torus"></a>
<div id="thm:torus" class="theorem">

<a id="eq:log-volume-growth"></a>
<a id="eq:crossing-chart"></a>
**Theorem 4** (A special Lagrangian torus near a maximal crossing). *Let $\pi:\mathcal Y\to\Delta$ be the restriction to a disk of a projective algebraic family over a smooth curve. Assume that $\mathcal Y$ is smooth, $\mathcal Y_0$ is a reduced simple normal crossings divisor, and the fibers $Y_t$ for $t\ne0$ are smooth connected projective manifolds of complex dimension $d\ge2$ with trivial canonical bundle. Let $a_t$ be polarizing classes given by a line bundle on $\mathcal Y$; this line bundle need not be relatively ample at $0$. Let $\eta_t$ be nowhere vanishing holomorphic $d$-forms which extend as a section of the relative logarithmic canonical line bundle. Put $s=-\log|t|$, and suppose that, for constants $c,C>0$, $$c s^d\le I_t:=\int_{Y_t}|\eta_t|^2\le C s^d,
 \qquad 0<|t|\ll1.
 \label{eq:log-volume-growth}$$ Suppose, moreover, that at some point $o\in\mathcal Y_0$ exactly $d+1$ components meet and that the logarithmic coefficient of $\eta$ at $o$ is nonzero. Thus in a coordinate neighborhood of $o$, $$z_0z_1\cdots z_d=t,
 \qquad
 \eta_t=f(z)\,\frac{\mathrm dz_1}{z_1}\wedge\cdots\wedge
                         \frac{\mathrm dz_d}{z_d},
 \qquad f(o)\ne0.
 \label{eq:crossing-chart}$$ Equip $Y_t$ with the Ricci-flat Kähler metric in $a_t$. For every sufficiently small nonzero $t$, the fiber $Y_t$ contains a smooth embedded special Lagrangian real $d$-torus isotopic in $Y_t$ to an angular coordinate torus in <a href="#eq:crossing-chart" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:crossing-chart">[eq:crossing-chart]</a>. Here special Lagrangian means that the Kähler form restricts to zero and that $\eta_t$ has constant phase on the oriented torus.*

</div>

We begin with an arbitrary sequence of nonzero parameters tending to zero. The compactness argument will produce a subsequence on which every sufficiently late fiber contains the required torus. At the end of Section <a href="#sec:completion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:completion">3</a>, a sequence of counterexamples will rule out failure anywhere in a sufficiently small punctured disk.

This section chooses centers with noncollapsed unit balls and controls the metric and logarithmic coordinates on every ball about them. Section <a href="#sec:completion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:completion">3</a> identifies the pointed limit as a smooth flat cylinder and perturbs its angular torus. All constants below are independent of $t$ in a sufficiently small punctured disk, unless stated otherwise. Every extraction is from the arbitrary initial sequence.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### Coordinate volume and mixed intersections

<a id="eq:torus-scaling"></a>
Let $g_t^{(0)}$ and $\omega_t^{(0)}$ be the Ricci-flat metric and Kähler form supplied by the Calabi–Yau theorem (Yau 1978) <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[Yau](#ref-Yau)], with $[\omega_t^{(0)}]=a_t$. The useful scale is $$g_t=s g_t^{(0)},\qquad \omega_t=s\omega_t^{(0)},\qquad
 \mu_t=\mathrm dV_t=\frac{\omega_t^d}{d!},\qquad
 \mu_t(Y_t)=c_a s^d,
 \quad c_a=\frac1{d!}\int_{Y_t}a_t^d>0.
 \label{eq:torus-scaling}$$ The last constant is independent of $t$ by topological local triviality over the punctured disk. Scaling does not change either of the special Lagrangian conditions.

Take $z_1,\ldots,z_d$ to be algebraic local equations of the corresponding components. They extend to rational functions on the algebraic total space. The remaining unit in the local equation of $\pi$ can be absorbed into $z_0$; no rationality of this last coordinate will be used. Shrink the analytic coordinate neighborhood so that $|z_i|<r_*<1$ there. On its smooth fibers set $$x_i=\log|z_i|,\qquad y_i=\arg z_i\in\mathbb R/(2\pi\mathbb Z),\qquad
 \gamma=\sum_{i=1}^d(\mathrm dx_i^2+\mathrm dy_i^2),\qquad
 \omega_\gamma=\sum_{i=1}^d\mathrm dx_i\wedge\mathrm dy_i.$$ Thus $x_i+iy_i$ are local holomorphic logarithms, and $\gamma$ is the standard flat metric on $\mathbb R^d\times(\mathbb R/2\pi\mathbb Z)^d$. Its angular circles have fixed length; we will compare $g_t$ with this metric. Let $$\Sigma=\left\{b\in\mathbb R^d:b_i<0\ (1\le i\le d),\quad
                              \sum_i b_i>-1\right\}.$$ For a relatively compact subset $B\Subset\Sigma$, write $$\mathcal T_t(B)=\{x/s\in B,\quad y\in(\mathbb R/2\pi\mathbb Z)^d\}.$$ For small $t$ this tube is contained in the coordinate neighborhood. Indeed all $|z_i|$, including $|z_0|=\exp(-s-\sum_i x_i)$, are bounded by $e^{-\delta_B s}$ for some $\delta_B>0$. In particular, these tubes converge uniformly to $o$ in the total space.

<a id="eq:coordinate-density"></a>
Use the convention $|\eta_t|^2=2^{-d}i^{d^2}\eta_t\wedge\overline{\eta_t}$, so that <a href="#eq:crossing-chart" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:crossing-chart">[eq:crossing-chart]</a> gives $|\eta_t|^2=|f(z)|^2\mathrm dV_\gamma$ in the chart. Ricci-flatness implies that its volume form is a constant multiple of $|\eta_t|^2$; integration determines that constant. Hence $$\mathrm dV_t=\frac{c_a s^d}{I_t}|\eta_t|^2,
 \qquad
 \frac{\mathrm dV_t}{\mathrm dV_\gamma}
       =\frac{c_a s^d}{I_t}|f(z)|^2
       \longrightarrow\vartheta>0
 \quad\hbox{uniformly on }\mathcal T_t(B)
 \label{eq:coordinate-density}$$ after a subsequence. To obtain the convergence, first extract a limit of $c_a s^d/I_t$ using <a href="#eq:log-volume-growth" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:log-volume-growth">[eq:log-volume-growth]</a>, and then use $f(z)\to f(o)\ne0$. In particular, the two volume measures are uniformly comparable on each fixed interior tube.

<a id="stmt-lem-mixed-estimates"></a>
<div id="lem:mixed-estimates" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:mixed-estimates"></a>
